Let's design an optimizer:
- Represent sequence a positive (avoid zeros maybe eps) length n, normalized sum=1.
- Score = 2n * max conv (computed with FFT) / sum^2 (sum=1). We'll use sum=1 for optimization.
- Objective smooth J = logsumexp(β c) / β, maybe plus small max of c? We can use c = a*a. Since a sum 1, c values O(1/n), β maybe 50-200. logsumexp with c*β.
- Gradient wrt log a: For a -> exp(x)/sum? If we keep a normalized by dividing by sum after updates, gradient of J wrt x_j = a_j * ∂J/∂a_j (ignoring normalization) because a_j = exp(x_j). If we renormalize, approximate. Or work with a directly and use mirror step: a ← a * exp(-lr * grad_a / T)? Then normalize. Simpler: use gradient ascent on entropy? Let's do multiplicative update: `a = a * exp(-lr * grad_a)`, clip min, normalize. grad_a from smooth max. This is like natural gradient, maintains positivity.
- Choose lr small, adapt based on improvement? Could use Adam on x: x=log(a+eps); grad_x = a*grad_a; Adam update; a=softmax(x) (i.e., exp(x)/sum). This is stable but softmax can underflow for large length; use x - max.

Let's design a strong `local_search(a)`:
- Input positive array length n. We'll normalize sum=1.
- Use FFT convolution function `conv_score(a)` returning (score, max_c, c). For speed, use real FFT length `nfft = 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()`.
- Compute initial score.
- Iterate until time budget:
   * Compute c = conv(a) (FFT). M=max(c[0:2n-1]).
   * Choose weights w = softmax(β c / T?) Let's use normalized active weights: `t = c / max(c)`; `w = (t ** p) / sum(t**p)` with p maybe 8-64. This gives weights to top peaks; gradient of F = ∑ w_k c_k (not log). Since weights sum 1, F approximates max. Gradient = 2 cross-correlation(a,w). Use this for step. It is smooth-ish, no overflow.
   * Update in natural gradient: `a_new = a * exp(-lr * grad_a)` (or additive). Then normalize to sum1.
   * We want minimize F. But F = ∑ w c, gradient points increase c. Step down. Use line search? Could just fixed lr schedule.
   * Also occasionally perform random restarts / perturbations.
- Track best true score (compute exact/FFT true max). Since FFT convolution same as true (within fp). Use internal.

Will natural gradient converge to local minima? Maybe. We can also use **Adam on log a**:
   x = log(a + eps). grad_x = a * grad_a. Adam update x, a=softmax(x). This adapts.
Need choose lr ~0.01? Since grad_a magnitude: c ~1/n, w sum1, grad =2 cross. For uniform a=1/n, w on center, grad ~2*(1/n)*count? Let's estimate grad magnitude O(1/n). lr 0.1 maybe. Use adaptive: lr = 1e-3 * n? We can tune by monitoring.

Maybe simpler: Use **projected gradient on simplex** with gradient of p-norm. Set a positive, update a -= lr*grad; then project to simplex (water filling). Projection O(n log n). But zeros may appear; reset small to eps. Natural gradient likely better.

Let's derive gradient formula implementation with FFT robustly.
We need grad_j = 2 ∑_{i=0}^{n-1} a_i w_{j+i}, where w_k defined for k=0..2n-2, else 0. This is cross-correlation of a with w at nonnegative lags. We can compute using `np.correlate` for n<=2000 maybe okay if many? For n=5000, O(25M) per iter too slow. Use FFT.
Find FFT formula. Define arrays length N >= 2n-1.
Let A = fft(a padded), W = fft(w padded). We want b_j = ∑_i a_i w_{j+i}. This is like convolution of a with reversed/negated w. Let w_neg[t] = w_{-t mod N} = w_{N-t} for t>0? Then (a * w_neg)[j] = ∑_i a_i w_neg[j-i] = ∑_i a_i w_{i-j}. That's ∑ a_i w_{i-j}, not w_{j+i}. If use w_pos_rev? Let's test with small. We want for j=0: b0 = a0 w0 + a1 w1 + ... + a_{n-1} w_{n-1}. This is dot(a, w[0:n]). For j=1: a0 w1 + a1 w2 + ... + a_{n-2} w_{n-1}+a_{n-1} w_n? (if exists). This is dot(a, w[1:n+1]). Generally b_j = inner product of a with w shifted by j. This is cross-correlation `corr[w, a]` at lag j: ∑ a_i w_{i+j}. In numpy `np.correlate(w, a, mode='full')`? Definition `corr[k] = ∑_n w[n+k] * conjugate(a[n])`? According to numpy, `correlate(a, v, 'full')` returns `∑ a[n] * conj(v[n+k])`? Let's recall: `np.correlate([1,2,3], [0,1,0.5])`? Hard. We can avoid by using FFT with reversed w.
Cross-correlation r[l] = ∑_n x[n+l] y[n]. It can be computed as ifft(conj(fft(x))*fft(y)) maybe. We want r[j]=∑_i w_j+i * a_i. Let x=w, y=a. So r = correlate(w,a). Using FFT: r = ifft( conj(fft(w)) * fft(a) )? For circular cross-correlation, (x ⋆ y)[k] = ∑_n x[n+k] y[n] = ifft( conj(FFTx) * FFty ). Yes. So compute `grad_half = ifft( conj(fft(w,N)) * fft(a,N) )` (real). Then b_j = grad_half[j] for j=0..n-1 (circular, but if N>=2n-2 and a,w zero-padded, circular shifts don't wrap? Need ensure w length 2n-2, a length n; x[n+k] for k up to n-1 and n up to n-1 => index up to 2n-2, within N. Good). Then grad_a = 2*real(grad_half[0:n]). We can verify orientation by finite difference in code for first few steps and if sign/orientation wrong maybe objective increases; can auto-correct by testing a random sequence: compute gradient numerical and dot with small perturbation? Could implement a self-test: for small n=5, create random a, compute analytic grad via direct O(n^2), compare to FFT; if max diff large, adjust by reversing w? But in final code, we can just use direct for n<=500 maybe to be safe; for large, use FFT with validated formula.

Let's derive direct gradient: For j in 0..n-1, grad[j]=0; for k in 0..2n-2: idx=k-j; if 0<=idx<n: grad[j]+=2*w[k]*a[idx]. That's O(n*2n)=O(n^2). For n=1000, 2M operations per iter maybe okay in Python if vectorized? Could use loop over k and update slicing: For each k, for j=max(0,k-n+1)..min(n-1,k), grad[j]+=2*w[k]*a[k-j]. This is O(number pairs) ~ n^2/2. In Python loops over n with vector slices maybe n=200 -> 20k, ok; n=5000 -> 25M slices too slow. Use FFT.

FFT cross-correlation: Let N >= 2n-1. `fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)`, `fw = np.fft.rfft(w, N)`. Then circular cross-correlation? For real, ifft(fa * conj(fw)) gives ∑ a[n] w[n+k] or w[n-k]. Let's test mentally with a=[1,2], w=[10,20,30,40] N=4. We want b0=1*10+2*20=50, b1=1*20+2*30=80.
Compute fa, fw. The linear convolution a * reverse(w) maybe. np.fft convolution property: ifft(fa*fw) = circular convolution a * w = c_k=∑ a_i w_{k-i}. At k=0: a0w0+a1w_{-1}=10+0=10; k=1: a0w1+a1w0=20+20=40; k=2: a0w2+a1w1=30+40=70; not b.
If use reverse(w): wr = [40,30,20,10]. ifft(fa*fwr): k=0:1*40+2*10=60; k=1:1*30+2*40=110; no.
Cross-correlation via ifft(fa*conj(fw)): Since w real, conj=fw. Same as convolution? Wait convolution property: FFT of convolution = product. Correlation = product with one conj. But for real, conj doesn't change in frequency? It does change phase conjugate, not same if complex. We computed fa*fw (not conj) for convolution. fa*conj(fw) is cross-correlation. Need values. Let's compute DFT maybe too long. Known formula: `corr = ifft(fa * conj(fw))` yields `∑ a[n] conj(w[n+m])`? At m=0: ∑ a[n]w[n]=1*10+2*20=50. Good maybe. At m=1: ∑ a[n] w[n+1]=1*20+2*30=80. So desired. So `b = irfft(fa * np.conj(fw), N)[:n]`. Great. For real, conj matters. We'll use that.

Convolution c = a*a: `fc = fft(a,N)`, `c = irfft(fc*fc, N)[:2n-1]` (not conj). Good.

Optimization details:
- We'll normalize sum to 1. For true score: score = 2*n*max(c)/sum^2, after sum=1, score=2*n*max(c).
- For weights: `c_pos = np.clip(c,0,None)` (FFT may tiny negative). `M = c_pos.max()`. If M <=0 return inf. Use p-norm weights: choose p=16 maybe. `t = c_pos / M`; `w = t**p`; `ws=w.sum()`; if ws>0 w/=ws else uniform. This emphasizes top peaks. p can increase over time. But gradient of ∑w c with fixed w is ok. This is like trust region. We can update w each iteration.
- Step: natural gradient descent: `a = a * exp(-step * grad)` with grad = 2*b. But grad can be large for zeros. Use log: `x = log(a)`, `grad_x = a * grad_a` (since da/dx=a). Then maybe Adam.
Implement Adam on x:
   x = np.log(a + 1e-30)
   m = v = 0
   for it:
      c=conv(a); M=...
      w=...
      b=corr(w,a) via FFT; grad_a=2*b
      grad_x = a * grad_a
      Adam: t, beta1=0.9, beta2=0.99, eps=1e-8
      m = b1*m+(1-b1)*grad_x; v=b2*v+(1-b2)*grad_x^2
      mhat=m/(1-b1^t); vhat=v/(1-b2^t)
      x -= lr * mhat / (sqrt(vhat)+eps)

Let's design exact score internal:
```
def fft_conv(a):
   n=len(a); N=1; while N<2*n-1: N*=2
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
   c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:2*n-1]
   return c
```
For very large n (6962), N=16384 or 32768? 2n-1=13923, N=16384. Good.

Score:
```
def internal_score(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   s=a.sum()
   if s<0.01: return inf
   c=fft_conv(a)
   M=np.max(c)
   return 2*len(a)*M/(s*s)
```
FFT errors may make M slightly negative? Clip.

For final evaluate_sequence uses np.convolve O(n^2) in C; for n=6962 maybe 48M multiplications, maybe 0.2s. Fine.

Custom Adam on x:
```
def adam_logsearch(a, time_limit, start_time, beta_factor, lr, p=None):
    n=a.size
    x=np.log(a+1e-30)
    m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0
    best=a.copy(); best_score=internal_score(a)
    beta = beta_factor*n
    while time.monotonic()-start_time < time_limit:
       c=fft_conv(a)
       M=np.max(c[0:2*n-1]+1e-300)
       if M<=0: break
       z=beta*c[:2*n-1]; z-=z.max(); e=np.exp(z); w=e/e.sum()
       b=cross_corr(w,a)
       grad_a=2*b
       grad_x=a*grad_a
       t+=1
       m=0.9*m+0.1*grad_x
       v=0.99*v+0.01*grad_x*grad_x
       mhat=m/(1-0.9**t); vhat=v/(1-0.99**t)
       x = x - lr*mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-8)
       # maybe periodic renormalize x -= mean to avoid growth
       x -= np.mean(x)
       a = softmax(x)
       score=2*n*np.max(fft_conv? we already have c? but c old; need new score maybe approximate use FFT again)
       if score<best: ...
```

```

Let's design a **grid optimizer for the continuous q function**:
- Use b bins (e.g., 50) values. Initialize as power law sampled in bins.
- Use multi-start Adam/LBFGS on bin values to minimize true score of expanded sequence of length n=b*r (r e.g., 16 or 32). Since b small, L-BFGS feasible. This can find q modifications.
- Then take best bin values, expand to length n (e.g., 256/512) and run full Adam.

Let's integrate LP improvement:
```
def lp_improve(a, max_solves, time_budget):
   for solve in range(max_solves):
      if time out break
      a = normalize(a, sum=1)
      c=conv(a); M=max(c)
      build C (dense) maybe for n<=300
      solve LP max sum g s.t. C g <= M, 0<=g<=1000
      if success and sum_g > 1:
         g=res.x; if finite
         g = clip(g,0,1000); s=g.sum(); if s>1:
             for t in [0.5,1.0,0.25,0.75]:
                cand=(1-t)*a+t*g/s
                score=internal_score(cand)
                if score<best: accept; break
      else break
   return best
```

Let's outline `lp_sequential(a)`:
```
def lp_sequential(a, max_time, n_lp_limit=512):
    a=normalize(a, np.sqrt(2*n))
    best=a; bestM=scoreM(a)
    for it in range(max_iter):
       if time left <5 break
       M=bestM
       A = build_convolution_rows(best)
       res=linprog(c=-1, A_ub=A, b_ub=M*ones, bounds=(0,1000), method='highs', options={'presolve': True, 'dual_feasibility_tolerance':1e-9, 'primal_feasibility_tolerance':1e-9})
       if not res.success: break
       g=np.clip(res.x,0,1000)
       s=g.sum()
       if s <= best_sum*(1+1e-6): break
       g=g*best_sum/s
       # line search blend
       best_candidate=best; bestM_cur=bestM
       for t in [0.5,0.25,0.75,1.0,0.1,0.9,0.33,0.67,0.05]:
           cand=(1-t)*best + t*g
           cand=np.clip(cand,0,1000)
           cand_sum=cand.sum()
           if cand_sum<0.01: continue
           # normalize to best_sum? blend of normalized sum S already S if g sum S; clipping may alter. Normalize.
           cand = cand * (best_sum / cand.sum())
           M_new = scoreM(cand)
           if M_new < bestM_cur:
               bestM_cur=M_new; best_candidate=cand
       best=best_candidate
       if bestM >= bestM_prev + tol: maybe no improvement break
    return best
```
But note line search using g from best before blend; after choose best, next LP uses new best. Good.

The LP A rows use `best` values maybe not normalized? Use normalized. For k, row[j]=best[k-j] if valid. For linprog, A shape (m,n). m=2n-1. This is dense. We can build efficiently:
```
def build_A(a):
    n=len(a)
    A=np.empty((2*n-1,n), dtype=a.dtype)
    # For each k, we can fill zeros then valid slice reversed.
    A.fill(0.0)
    for k in range(2*n-1):
       lo=max(0,k-n+1); hi=min(n-1,k)+1
       idx=np.arange(k, k-(hi-lo), -1)
       A[k, lo:hi]=a[idx]
    return A
```

return c
```

Since L>n-1, yes. No overlap with low segment? Low segment 0..n-1 and high segment N-L+0..N-1. They may overlap if n-1 >= N-L, i.e., L+n-1 >= N. We choose N as power of two >=2n-1; L+n-1=3n-3. For n=2, N=4, L+n-1=3<4 okay. For n=3, N=8 (need5), L=4, L+n-1=6<8. For n=4, N=8? need7, N=8, L=6, L+n-1=9 >8, overlap! Then circular convolution low and high segments overlap, corrupting. Need N large enough so low and high segments don't overlap: N > L + n -1 = 3n-3. Thus choose N >= 3n-1 for gradient via w_neg convolution. That's larger (next pow2 ~4n). For n=512, N=2048? 3n=1536, pow2 2048. Fine. For convolution c we need N>=2n-1. For gradient, use N_grad maybe next pow2 >=3n-1 to be safe. Or we can avoid by padding w_neg differently? If assign w_neg length L+1 with nonzero at positions 0 and L..? But in FFT can create array of length N with high indices at position L (not N-k) to do linear convolution: define `w_rev_shift[p] = w[L-p]?` Let's find a way with N>=2n-1.
We want b_j=∑ a_i w_{i+j}. This is cross-correlation. We can compute linear correlation by convolution of a with reversed w shifted. Let w_rev of length L+1: w_rev[p]=w[L-p] for p=0..L. Then convolution a * w_rev: D[k]=∑ a_i w_rev[k-i]=∑ a_i w[L-k+i]. Set k = L - j: D[L-j]=∑ a_i w[j+i]. Good! So choose `w_rev = w[::-1]` length L+1 (2n-1). Let N_conv >= n+L = 3n-2? Alternatively, use `np.fft.fft` not rfft? Use rfft.
Implementation:
```
def positive_lag_corr(w, a):
   n=len(a); L=2*n-2
   N=1
   minN = 3*n - 1  # safe
   while N<minN: N<<=1
   # w length L+1 = 2n-1
   wrev = np.zeros(N)
   wrev[:L+1] = w[::-1]
   a_arr=np.zeros(N); a_arr[:n]=a
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a_arr)
   fw=np.fft.rfft(wrev)
   conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw, N)
   # D[k] for k=0..n+L-2. Need k=L-j? L=2n-2. Wait w length L+1, w_rev[p]=w[L-p]. D[L-j]=∑ a_i w_rev[L-j-i]=∑ a_i w[j+i] if 0<=L-j-i<=L. For j=0: k=L, D[L]=∑ a_i w[i]? Let's test with n=2, L=2, w=[10,20,30], wrev=[30,20,10]. Conv a=[1,2] * wrev: D0=30, D1=1*20+2*30=80, D2=1*10+2*20=50. Need b0=50 at k=L=2, b1=80 at k=1. So b_j=conv[L-j]. Good.
   b=np.array([conv[L-j] for j in range(n)]) -> for j=0 conv2, j=1 conv1. Use slice: conv[L:L-n:-1]? For n=2, L=2: conv[2:0:-1] -> [50,80]? In Python conv[2:0:-1] gives indices 2,1 yes. For j=0..n-1, `conv[L:L-n:-1]` length n.
```
But linear convolution computed with N>=n+L. For n=2, minN=5, pow2 8, ok.
We can also test with direct in code for small n and if mismatch, but not necessary.

Gradient of smooth objective with weights w normalized: `grad_a = 2 * positive_lag_corr(w, a)`.

Let's verify with simple n=2, a=[x,y], c=[x^2,2xy,y^2]. Objective F=w0 x^2 + w1 2xy + w2 y^2. ∂F/∂x=2w0 x +2w1 y. Our formula 2∑_k w_k a_{k-j}. For j=0: k=0 a0=x ->w0 x; k=1 a1=y ->w1 y; k=2 a? out. Gives 2(w0 x + w1 y). Correct. j=1: k=1 a0=x? k-j=0 ->w1 x; k=2 a1=y ->w2 y =>2(w1 x + w2 y). Correct. Good.
So positive_lag_corr(w,a) j gives ∑ w_k a_{k-j}. Yes.

a=normalize_to_sum(a,S)
x=np.log(a+1e-30); x-=np.mean(x)
m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); t=0; best_score=fast_M(a); best=a.copy()
lr=0.05? 
no_improve=0
while time < limit:
   c=conv(a); M=max(c)
   if M<best_score: best=a; best_score=M; no_improve=0; maybe increase lr? else no_improve++
   # adjust beta over iterations: beta = beta_start + (beta_end-beta_start)*min(1, it/iters)
   # weights
   z=beta*c; z-=z.max(); e=np.exp(z); w=e/e.sum()
   b=pos_corr(w,a)
   grad_a=2*b
   grad_x=a*grad_a
   # Adam
   t+=1; ...
   x -= lr*mhat/(sqrt(vhat)+1e-12)
   x -= np.mean(x)
   # if x range too large, clamp
   a=softmax(x)
   # If a has many tiny, maybe mix with best occasionally
   if no_improve > 1000:
       # random restart from best with perturbation
       a = best * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.05,size=n)); normalize
       x=log(a); reset m,v? 
       no_improve=0
return best
```

def build_convolution_matrix(a):

def simple_candidates(n, rng):
   candidates=[]
   # power variants
   i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
   for gamma in [0.25,0.5,0.75,1.0,1.5]:
      for alpha in [0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6]:
         a=(i+gamma)**(-alpha)
         if alpha>0.55 maybe a0 large; still positive.
         candidates.append(a)
   # two block
   a=np.ones(n); a[n//2:]=2; candidates.append(a)
   # three block [1,1,2]? Maybe ratios [1,1.333,2.666]
   # interpolate [3,4,8]
   m=len([...]); x=np.linspace(0,1,n); y=np.interp(x, np.linspace(0,1,m), np.log(seq)); a=np.exp(y)
   # random smooth: base power * exp(sum cos)
   for trial in range(3):
      amp=0.1*rng...
   return candidates
```

Let's define `candidate_score(a)`:
```
def candidate_score(a):
    a=np.asarray(a,dtype=float)
    a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s=a.sum()
    if s<0.01 or not np.isfinite(s): return float('inf')
    c=fft_convolve(a)
    m=float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m<0 and m>-1e-9: m=0.0
    return float(2*len(a)*m/(s*s))
```
For normalized sequences, score=m if s=S. Use this for initial.

`normalize_to_sum(a, target)` returns None if invalid. It should clip at 1000; if after scaling values >1000, clip and renormalize; if sum becomes 0 return None. Since target ~√(2n) < 1000 for n<5e4, clipping unlikely.

`fast_conv_max(a)` assumes valid positive; compute c. Use FFT with `N=1; while N < 2*len(a)-1: N<<=1`. For very small n<32 use `np.convolve` for exact maybe.
```
def fft_convolve(a):
   n=len(a)
   if n <= 128:
       return np.convolve(a,a,mode='full')[:2*n-1]
   N=1; need=2*n-1; while N<need: N<<=1
   fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N); return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:need]
```
Direct for n=128 O16k, okay. For many Adam iterations n=128 direct 16k*100k=1.6e9 maybe too high. Use FFT for n>32 perhaps. Let's set direct only n<=20.

`positive_lag_corr` uses N>=3n-1. For n small direct:
```
def positive_lag_corr(w,a):
   n=len(a)
   if n<=64:
       b=np.zeros(n)
       for j in range(n):
           # w indices j..j+n-1, but w length 2n-1. Use slice
           b[j]=np.dot(a, w[j:j+n])
       return b
   N=1; minN=3*n-1; while N<minN: N<<=1
   wrev=np.zeros(N); L=2*n-2; wrev[:L+1]=w[::-1]
   arr=np.zeros(N); arr[:n]=a
   f=np.fft.rfft(arr); g=np.fft.rfft(wrev)
   conv=np.fft.irfft(f*g,N)
   return conv[L:L-n:-1].copy()
```
Direct O(n^2), for n=64 4k, ok if not many. For Adam n=256 use FFT minN=767 ->1024. Good.
Need ensure w length exactly 2n-1. In weights, c length 2n-1, after softmax length same. In direct slice `w[j:j+n]` for j=n-1 => w[n-1:2n-1] length n, ok.

Test direct vs FFT mentally with n=2, w=[10,20,30], a=[1,2], L=2. wrev=[30,20,10], conv linear? With N=8, conv coefficients: D0=30,D1=80,D2=50. `conv[L:L-n:-1]` = conv[2:0:-1] -> [conv2,conv1]=[50,80]. Good.

`build_convolution_matrix(a)` column fill with zeros. For n=0? not.

`lp_improve(a, deadline, max_solves=20)`:
Inputs normalized to S? We'll normalize inside.
```
def lp_improve(a, deadline, max_solves=20, n_limit=1024):
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    n=a.size
    if n==0 or n>n_limit: return a
    target=np.sqrt(2.0*n)
    a=normalize_to_sum(a,target)
    if a is None: return None
    c,M=fast_conv_max(a)
    best=a.copy(); bestM=M
    improve_count=0
    for it in range(max_solves):
       if time.monotonic()>deadline-2: break
       # floor a
       a_lp=np.maximum(best,1e-8)
       a_lp*=target/a_lp.sum()
       A=build_convolution_matrix(a_lp)
       b_ub=np.full(2*n-1, M*(1+1e-9)+1e-12)
       cobj=-np.ones(n)
       bounds=np.zeros((n,2)); bounds[:,1]=1000.0
       try:
          res=linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=b_ub, bounds=bounds, method='highs')
       except Exception:
          break
       if res is None or not getattr(res,'success',False): break
       g=np.asarray(res.x,float)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)): break
       g=np.clip(g,0,1000)
       gs=g.sum()
       if not np.isfinite(gs) or gs <= target*(1+1e-8): break
       g*=target/gs
       # line search
       accepted=False
       # candidate steps descending maybe [1,0.5,0.25,0.75,0.1,0.9,0.33,0.67,0.05]
       steps=[1.0,0.5,0.25,0.75,0.1,0.9,0.333333,0.666667,0.05]
       current_best=best; current_bestM=bestM
       for t in steps:
          cand=(1-t)*best + t*g
          cand=np.clip(cand,1e-10,1000)
          cs=cand.sum()
          if cs<0.01: continue
          cand*=target/cs
          c_new,M_new=fast_conv_max(cand)
          if M_new < current_bestM - 1e-10:
              current_best=cand; current_bestM=M_new; current_c=c_new; accepted=True; # keep trying? maybe try all to find best
       if accepted:
          best=current_best; bestM=current_bestM; improve_count +=1
          # maybe update c,M? bestM
       else:
          # if no improvement, try random blend? or break after few
          if improve_count>0 and it>3: break
          # if never improve, break
          if it>5: break
    return best
```
We evaluate all steps to choose best; could only first but fine. This line search costs up to 9 FFTs per LP solve. If LP solved 10, ok. Could optimize by trying [1,0.5,0.25] only. But blend t=1 (g) often. Use steps [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.75,0.1]. Fine.

Note: `best` and `g` both sum target. After clip floor, cand sum target. Good.

Potential: LP may return g with sum slightly < target due to bounds/solver tolerance; break. If g has approximate same score, no improvement. We can try perturbing g? Not.

`max_solves` maybe 30. If a solve is very slow, time check after.

`adam_smooth(a, deadline)`:
Use normalized target S. We'll return best.
```
def adam_smooth(a, deadline):
   a=normalize_to_sum(a, sqrt(2n)); if None return None
   c,M=fast_conv_max(a)
   best=a.copy(); bestM=M; best_c=c
   x=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-30)); x-=np.mean(x)
   m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); it=0
   step=0.1; beta_start=20; beta_end=200
   rng=np.random.default_rng(123)
   no_improve=0; fail=0
   while time.monotonic()<deadline:
      it+=1
      if M<bestM:
         bestM=M; best=a.copy(); best_c=c.copy(); no_improve=0
      beta=beta_start + (beta_end-beta_start)*min(1.0, it/1000.0)
      # weights
      z=beta*c
      zmax=z.max(); if not finite:...
      e=np.exp(z-zmax); w=e/e.sum()
      b=positive_lag_corr(w,a)
      grad=a*(2*b)
      # If gradient norm zero break
      gnorm=np.linalg.norm(grad)
      if gnorm <1e-18: break
      it2=it+1
      m=0.9*m+0.1*grad
      v=0.99*v+0.01*grad*grad
      mhat=m/(1-0.9**it2)
      vhat=v/(1-0.99**it2)
      dir=-mhat/(np.sqrt(vhat)+1e-12)
      # normalize direction global: by RMS
      dir_norm=np.sqrt(np.mean(dir*dir))
      if dir_norm>0: dir=dir/dir_norm
      # trial steps
      accepted=False
      trial_step=step
      for _ in range(4):
          dx=trial_step*dir
          # clamp dx maybe
          maxdx=np.max(np.abs(dx))
          if maxdx>0.8: dx*=0.8/maxdx
          x_trial=x+dx
          a_trial=softmax(x_trial)
          c_trial,M_trial=fast_conv_max(a_trial)
          if M_trial < M - 1e-12:
             x=x_trial; a=a_trial; c=c_trial; M=M_trial; step=min(0.5, step*1.02); accepted=True; break
          trial_step*=0.5
      if not accepted:
         fail+=1
         step=max(1e-8, step*0.7)
         if fail>80:
             # reset to best with small perturbation
             a=np.maximum(best,1e-30)
             sigma=0.05 if bestM>1.5 else 0.02
             a=a*np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,size=n))
             a=normalize_to_sum(a,target)
             if a is None: break
             c,M=fast_conv_max(a)
             x=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-30)); x-=np.mean(x)
             m=np.zeros(n); v=np.zeros(n); step=0.05; fail=0
      else:
         fail=0
      # Periodically try a random block perturbation of best? maybe
      if it % 200 ==0 and M>bestM*1.001:
          # maybe skip
   return best
```
Softmax top-level:
```
def softmax(x):
   x=x-np.max(x); e=np.exp(x); return e/e.sum()
```
This recalculates sum; acceptable.

But `np.linalg.norm(grad)` O(n). Fine.

The Adam line search computes `fast_conv_max` up to 4 times per iteration. For n=512, each conv N=2048; 4* maybe 0.0003? Actually Python overhead. Could be okay for 1000 iterations. If deadline 200s, maybe 1000-5000 iter. Good.

Could be too slow due to positive_lag_corr FFT N=2048 plus conv N=2048 and trial convs. Maybe 3 FFTs per iteration. 10k iter ~30k FFTs, maybe 30s? Might be okay.

To reduce, use trial only every few iterations or one trial. We'll do up to 3 trials. Set `for _ in range(3)`.

Also, `fast_conv_max` uses FFT for each trial; if a_trial has tiny negative? softmax positive.

`upsample`:
```
def upsample(a, n):
   m=len(a)
   if m==n: return a.copy()
   x0=np.linspace(0,1,m); y=np.log(np.maximum(a,1e-30))
   x1=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   y1=np.interp(x1,x0,y)
   out=np.exp(y1 - np.max(y1))
   return out
```
Scale later.

Potential bug: `np.interp` with m=1; handle.

Functions:

`normalize_to_sum(a, target)`:
```
def normalize_to_sum(a, target):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return None
    a = a * (target / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s2 = float(a.sum())
    if s2 <= 0.0:
        return None
    a = a * (target / s2)
    return a
```
After second clip, sum may not exactly target but close. Could loop once? Not needed.

`fft_convolve(a)`:
```
def fft_convolve(a):
    n = len(a)
    if n <= 16:
        return np.convolve(a, a, mode='full')[:2*n-1]
    need = 2*n - 1
    N = 1
    while N < need:
        N <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    return c[:need]
```

`fast_conv_max(a)`:
```
def fast_conv_max(a):
    c = fft_convolve(a)
    # clip tiny negative
    if c.size:
        if np.any(c < 0.0):
            c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
        M = float(np.max(c))
    else:
        M=0.0
    return c, M
```

`candidate_score(a)`:
```
def candidate_score(a):
   a=np.asarray(a,float)
   if a.ndim!=1: a=a.ravel()
   if not a.size: return inf
   a=np.clip(a,0,1000)
   if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)): return inf
   s=float(a.sum())
   if s<0.01: return inf
   c=fft_convolve(a)
   if np.any(c<0): c=np.maximum(c,0)
   M=float(np.max(c))
   if not np.isfinite(M): return inf
   return float(2.0 * len(a) * M / (s*s))
```

`softmax_vec(x)`:
```
def softmax_vec(x):
   x=np.asarray(x,float); x=x-np.max(x); e=np.exp(x); return e/e.sum()
```

`positive_lag_corr(w,a)`:
```
def positive_lag_corr(w, a):
    n=len(a)
    L=2*n-2
    w=np.asarray(w,float)
    if w.size < L+1:
       w2=np.zeros(L+1); w2[:w.size]=w; w=w2
    else:
       w=w[:L+1]
    if n <= 64:
       b=np.empty(n)
       for j in range(n):
           b[j]=float(np.dot(a, w[j:j+n]))
       return b
    minN=3*n-1
    N=1
    while N<minN: N<<=1
    wrev=np.zeros(N)
    wrev[:L+1]=w[::-1]
    arr=np.zeros(N); arr[:n]=a
    fa=np.fft.rfft(arr)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(wrev)
    conv=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw, N)
    return conv[L:L-n:-1].copy()
```
Check slice when n=1: L=0, L-n = -1, conv[0:-1:-1] empty? For n=1, direct used n<=64. Fine.

`build_convolution_matrix(a)`:
```
def build_convolution_matrix(a):
    n=len(a)
    A=np.zeros((2*n-1,n), dtype=np.float64)
    if n==0: return A
    for j in range(n):
       A[j:j+n, j]=a
    return A
```

```
def make_starts(n, rng):
    out=[]
    target=math.sqrt(2.0*n)
    if n <= 2:
       a=np.array([1.0,2.0][:n],float)
       b=normalize_to_sum(a,target); if b is not None: out.append(b)
       # [1,1]
    i=np.arange(n,float)
    gammas=(0.3,0.5,0.7,1.0,1.5)
    alphas=(0.4,0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6,0.7)
    for gamma in gammas:
      for alpha in alphas:
         a=(i+gamma)**(-alpha)
         b=normalize_to_sum(a,target)
         if b is not None: out.append(b)
    # two block
    for r in (1.0,1.5,2.0,2.5):
       a=np.ones(n)
       if n>1: a[n//2:]=r
       b=normalize_to_sum(a,target); if b: out.append(b)
    # interpolation [3,4,8]
    if n>1:
       xs=np.array([0.0,0.5,1.0])
       ys=np.log(np.array([3.0,4.0,8.0]))
       x=np.linspace(0,1,n)
       y=np.interp(x,xs,ys)
       a=np.exp(y)
       b=normalize_to_sum(a,target); if b: out.append(b)
       # [1,1,2,2]
       xs2=np.array([0.0,0.5,0.5,1.0])? np.interp requires increasing xp; use [0,1/3,2/3,1], values [1,1,2,2]
    # random smooth
    base=(i+0.5)**(-0.5)
    for trial in range(6):
       mod=np.zeros(n)
       for k in range(1,4):
           amp=0.03 + rng.random()*0.12
           ph=rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0*math.pi)
           if n>1:
              mod += amp*np.cos(2.0*math.pi*k*i/(n-1.0)+ph)
          else: mod[0]=0
       a=base*np.exp(mod)
       b=normalize_to_sum(a,target); if b: out.append(b)
    return out
```

`upsample_seq(a, n)`:
```
def upsample_seq(a, n):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); m=len(a)
    if m==n: return a.copy()
    if m==1:
       return np.full(n, a[0])
    a=np.maximum(a,1e-30)
    x0=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    y0=np.log(a)
    y0=y0 - np.mean(y0)
    x1=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    y1=np.interp(x1,x0,y0)
    return np.exp(y1)
```

def random_block_search(a, deadline, rng):
  a=normalize_target; best=a; bestM=score
  while time<deadline:
     cand=best.copy()
     width=max(2,int(rng.integers(n//100, n//5)))
     start=int(rng.integers(0,n-width))
     cand[start:start+width] *= math.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1))

```python
"""
Search algorithm:
- Starts from power-law and structured initializations.
- Uses the LP tangent-space update from the previous state of the art.
- Polishes with a log-domain smooth-max Adam method and random block search.
- Returns the best non-negative sequence found by the evaluation function.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None


def normalize_to_sum(a, target):
    """Clip to non-negative finite values and scale to target sum."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return None
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return None
    a = a * (target / s)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s2 = float(a.sum())
    if s2 <= 0.0:
        return None
    a = a * (target / s2)
    return a


def fft_convolve(a):
    """Full linear self-convolution using FFT for speed."""
    n = len(a)
    if n <= 16:
        return np.convolve(a, a, mode="full")[:2 * n - 1]
    need = 2 * n - 1
    N = 1
    while N < need:
        N <<= 1
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)
    return c[:need]


def fast_conv_max(a):
    """Return convolution and its maximum (tiny negatives clipped)."""
    c = fft_convolve(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return c, 0.0
    if np.any(c < 0.0):
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        m = 0.0
    return c, m


def candidate_score(a):
    """Fast internal evaluation mirroring evaluate_sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.ndim != 1:
        a = a.ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(a)):
        return float("inf")
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    c = fft_convolve(a)
    if c.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    if np.any(c < 0.0):
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * len(a) * m / (s * s))


def softmax_vec(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    x = x - np.max(x)
    e = np.exp(x)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        return np.full_like(x, 1.0 / max(1, x.size))
    return e / s


def positive_lag_corr(w, a):
    """
    b[j] = sum_i a[i] w[j+i] for j=0..n-1.
    This is the positive-lag cross-correlation needed for the gradient.
    """
    n = len(a)
    L = 2 * n - 2
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    if w.size < L + 1:
        w2 = np.zeros(L + 1, dtype=np.float64)
        w2[:w.size] = w
        w = w2
    else:
        w = w[:L + 1].copy()

    if n <= 64:
        b = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
        for j in range(n):
            b[j] = float(np.dot(a, w[j:j + n]))
        return b

    minN = 3 * n - 1
    N = 1
    while N < minN:
        N <<= 1

    wrev = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
    wrev[:L + 1] = w[::-1]
    arr = np.zeros(N, dtype=np.float64)
    arr[:n] = a

    fa = np.fft.rfft(arr)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(wrev)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fa * fw, N)
    return conv[L:L - n:-1].copy()


def build_convolution_matrix(a):
    """
    Matrix A such that A @ g = a * g (linear convolution).
    A[k, j] = a[k-j] if valid, else 0.
    """
    n = len(a)
    A = np.zeros((2 * n - 1, n), dtype=np.float64)
    for j in range(n):
        A[j:j + n, j] = a
    return A


def select_top_sequences(items, k):
    """Return top k finite (score, sequence) pairs."""
    items = list(items)
    if not items:
        return []
    scores = [float(s) for s, _ in items]
    idx = np.argsort(scores)
    out = []
    for i in idx:
        s, a = items[i]
        if np.isfinite(s):
            out.append((s, a))
        if len(out) >= k:
            break
    return out


def add_candidate(pool, score, a, maxsize=12):
    """Keep a small pool of the best candidates found so far."""
    if a is None or not np.isfinite(score):
        return pool
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    pool.append((float(score), a))
    if len(pool) < maxsize:
        return pool
    scores = [float(s) for s, _ in pool]
    idx = np.argsort(scores)
    out = []
    for i in idx[:maxsize]:
        out.append(pool[i])
    return out


def upsample_seq(a, n):
    """Log-linear upsampling of a positive sequence."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    m = len(a)
    if m == n:
        return a.copy()
    if m == 1:
        return np.full(n, float(a[0]), dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.maximum(a, 1e-30)
    x0 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m)
    y0 = np.log(a)
    y0 = y0 - np.mean(y0)
    x1 = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)
    y1 = np.interp(x1, x0, y0)
    return np.exp(y1)


def make_starts(n, rng):
    """Generate diverse normalized starts for a fixed length."""
    out = []
    target = math.sqrt(2.0 * n)
    if target <= 0.0:
        return out

    if n <= 2:
        for base in ([1.0, 2.0], [1.0, 1.0]):
            a = np.asarray(base[:n], dtype=np.float64)
            b = normalize_to_sum(a, target)
            if b is not None:
                out.append(b)

    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)

    # One-sided power laws.  The 1/sqrt law is a very strong continuous seed.
    gammas = (0.3, 0.5, 0.7, 1.0, 1.5)
    alphas = (0.40, 0.45, 0.50, 0.55, 0.60, 0.70)
    for gamma in gammas:
        for alpha in alphas:
            a = (i + gamma) ** (-alpha)
            b = normalize_to_sum(a, target)
            if b is not None:
                out.append(b)

    # Simple two-block starts.
    for r in (1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 2.5):
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        if n > 1:
            a[n // 2:] = r
        b = normalize_to_sum(a, target)
        if b is not None:
            out.append(b)

    if n > 1:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n)

        # Upsampled small known shapes.
        xs1 = np.array([0.0, 0.5, 1.0], dtype=np.float64)
        ys1 = np.log(np.array([3.0, 4.0, 8.0], dtype=np.float64))
        a = np.exp(np.interp(x, xs1, ys1))
        b = normalize_to_sum(a, target)
        if b is not None:
            out.append(b)

        xs2 = np.array([0.0, 1.0 / 3.0, 2.0 / 3.0, 1.0], dtype=np.float64)
        ys2 = np.log(np.array([1.0, 1.0, 2.0, 2.0], dtype=np.float64))
        a = np.exp(np.interp(x, xs2, ys2))
        b = normalize_to_sum(a, target)
        if b is not None:
            out.append(b)

        # Smooth random modulations of the power-law seed.
        base = (i + 0.5) ** (-0.5)
        for _ in range(6):
            mod = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            for k in range(1, 4):
                amp = 0.03 + rng.random() * 0.12
                ph = rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * math.pi)
                mod += amp * np.cos(2.0 * math.pi * k * i / float(n - 1) + ph)
            a = base * np.exp(mod)
            b = normalize_to_sum(a, target)
            if b is not None:
                out.append(b)

    return out


def lp_improve(a, deadline, max_solves=20):
    """
    LP tangent-step improvement.

    For current a with sum sqrt(2n), M = max(a*a). Solve
        maximize sum(g)
        subject to (a*g)_k <= M, g >= 0.
    Rescale g to the same sum and blend into a.
    """
    if linprog is None:
        return a

    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    target = math.sqrt(2.0 * n)
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, target)
    if a is None:
        return a

    c_cur, bestM = fast_conv_max(a)
    best = a.copy()
    accepted = 0

    for _ in range(max_solves):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 2.0:
            break

        # A tiny floor keeps the LP bounded when some entries are extremely small.
        floor = 1e-5
        a_lp = np.maximum(best, floor)
        a_lp = normalize_to_sum(a_lp, target)
        if a_lp is None:
            break
        _, M_lp = fast_conv_max(a_lp)
        if not np.isfinite(M_lp) or M_lp <= 0.0:
            break

        A = build_convolution_matrix(a_lp)
        bub = np.full(2 * n - 1, M_lp * (1.0 + 1e-9) + 1e-12, dtype=np.float64)
        cobj = -np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        bounds = np.zeros((n, 2), dtype=np.float64)
        bounds[:, 1] = 1000.0

        try:
            res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=bub, bounds=bounds, method="highs")
        except Exception:
            try:
                res = linprog(cobj, A_ub=A, b_ub=bub, bounds=bounds, method="revised simplex")
            except Exception:
                break

        if res is None or not getattr(res, "success", False):
            break

        g = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
            break
        g = np.clip(g, 0.0, 1000.0)
        gs = float(g.sum())
        if not np.isfinite(gs) or gs <= target * (1.0 + 1e-8):
            break
        g = g * (target / gs)

        cur_best = best
        cur_bestM = bestM
        improved = False

        steps = (1.0, 0.5, 0.25, 0.75, 0.1, 0.9, 0.333333, 0.666667, 0.05)
        for t in steps:
            cand = (1.0 - t) * best + t * g
            cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
            cs = float(cand.sum())
            if cs < 0.01:
                continue
            cand = cand * (target / cs)
            _, Mn = fast_conv_max(cand)
            if Mn < cur_bestM - 1e-10:
                cur_best = cand
                cur_bestM = Mn
                improved = True

        if improved:
            best = cur_best
            bestM = cur_bestM
            accepted += 1
        else:
            # If the tangent LP stops helping, try a little longer after a few
            # accepted moves, otherwise stop.
            if accepted > 0 and _ > 4:
                break
            if _ > 6:
                break

    return best


def adam_smooth(a, deadline):
    """
    Log-domain Adam smoothing of the maximum convolution coefficient.
    Works with sequences normalized to sum sqrt(2n), so max convolution is the score.
    """
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    target = math.sqrt(2.0 * n)
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, target)
    if a is None:
        return a

    rng = np.random.default_rng(12345)
    c, M = fast_conv_max(a)
    if not np.isfinite(M):
        return a

    best = a.copy()
    bestM = M

    x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-30))
    x = x - np.mean(x)
    m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    it = 0
    step = 0.10
    fail = 0
    beta_start = 20.0
    beta_end = 200.0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        it += 1

        if M < bestM:
            bestM = M
            best = a.copy()
            fail = 0

        if not np.isfinite(M):
            break

        beta = beta_start + (beta_end - beta_start) * min(1.0, float(it) / 1500.0)
        z = beta * c
        zmax = float(np.max(z))
        if not np.isfinite(zmax):
            break
        e = np.exp(z - zmax)
        ws = float(e.sum())
        if ws <= 0.0:
            w = np.full(2 * n - 1, 1.0 / (2 * n - 1), dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            w = e / ws

        b = positive_lag_corr(w, a)
        g_a = 2.0 * b

        # Exact gradient tangent to the simplex: grad_x = a * (g_a - weighted_mean)
        p = a / target
        avg = float(np.dot(p, g_a))
        grad_x = a * (g_a - avg)

        gn = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(grad_x * grad_x)))
        if gn <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(gn):
            break

        it2 = it + 1
        m = 0.9 * m + 0.1 * grad_x
        v = 0.99 * v + 0.01 * grad_x * grad_x
        mhat = m / (1.0 - 0.9 ** it2)
        vhat = v / (1.0 - 0.99 ** it2)
        direction = -mhat / (np.sqrt(vhat) + 1e-12)

        dn = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(direction * direction)))
        if dn <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(dn):
            break
        direction = direction / dn

        accepted_this = False
        trial_step = step
        for _ in range(3):
            dx = trial_step * direction
            maxdx = float(np.max(np.abs(dx)))
            if maxdx > 1.0:
                dx = dx * (1.0 / maxdx)
            x_trial = x + dx
            a_trial = target * softmax_vec(x_trial)
            c_trial, M_trial = fast_conv_max(a_trial)
            if M_trial < M - 1e-12:
                x = x_trial
                a = a_trial
                c = c_trial
                M = M_trial
                step = min(0.5, step * 1.03)
                accepted_this = True
                break
            trial_step *= 0.5

        if not accepted_this:
            fail += 1
            step = max(1e-8, step * 0.75)
            if fail > 90:
                # Restart from the incumbent with a small smooth perturbation.
                a = np.maximum(best, 1e-30)
                sigma = 0.04 if bestM > 1.55 else 0.02
                a = a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, n))
                a = normalize_to_sum(a, target)
                if a is None:
                    break
                c, M = fast_conv_max(a)
                x = np.log(np.maximum(a, 1e-30))
                x = x - np.mean(x)
                m = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                step = 0.05
                fail = 0
        else:
            fail = 0

    return best


def random_block_search(a, deadline, rng):
    """A simple block-multiplicative local search."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = len(a)
    if n == 0:
        return a
    target = math.sqrt(2.0 * n)
    a = normalize_to_sum(a, target)
    if a is None:
        return a

    _, bestM = fast_conv_max(a)
    best = a.copy()

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        width = int(max(2, rng.integers(max(2, n // 120), max(3, n // 6))))
        width = min(width, n)
        start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
        stop = min(n, start + width)

        cand = best.copy()
        cand[start:stop] *= math.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.10))
        cand = np.clip(cand, 0.0, 1000.0)
        cs = float(cand.sum())
        if cs < 0.01:
            continue
        cand = cand * (target / cs)

        _, Mn = fast_conv_max(cand)
        if Mn < bestM - 1e-12:
            best = cand
            bestM = Mn

    return best


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1500.0, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point.  Searches for a low-scoring non-negative sequence.
    """
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(60.0, float(budget_s) - 35.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    pool = []
    best_seq = np.array([1.0, 2.0], dtype=np.float64)
    best_score = candidate_score(best_seq)
    pool = add_candidate(pool, best_score, best_seq)

    def record(a, score):
        nonlocal best_seq, best_score
        if a is None or not np.isfinite(score):
            return
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
        pool_global = add_candidate(pool, score, a)
        if len(pool_global) != len(pool):
            pool = pool_global
        if score < best_score:
            best_score = float(score)
            best_seq = a.copy()
            print("new best:", float(score), "len:", int(a.size))

    # Initial quick scan over several lengths.
    scan_lengths = [8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256]
    for n in scan_lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 80.0:
            break

        starts = make_starts(n, rng)
        scored = []
        for a in starts:
            s = candidate_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(s):
                scored.append((s, a))
                pool = add_candidate(pool, s, a)
                if s < best_score:
                    best_score = float(s)
                    best_seq = a.copy()
                    print("new best:", float(s), "len:", int(n))

        top = select_top_sequences(scored, 3)

        # LP polishing for promising starts.  Keep this conservative early.
        if n <= 256 and len(top) > 0 and linprog is not None:
            for s0, a0 in top[:2]:
                if time.monotonic() > deadline - 60.0:
                    break
                budget = 0.25 * n + 5.0
                budget = min(budget, max(3.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 45.0))
                if budget < 5.0:
                    break
                a_lp = lp_improve(a0, time.monotonic() + budget, max_solves=max(1, int(n // 8) + 2))
                if a_lp is not None:
                    s_lp = candidate_score(a_lp)
                    pool = add_candidate(pool, s_lp, a_lp)
                    if s_lp < best_score:
                        best_score = float(s_lp)
                        best_seq = a_lp.copy()
                        print("new best after LP:", float(s_lp), "len:", int(n))

                # Light Adam polish on the LP result.
                if time.monotonic() < deadline - 45.0:
                    adam_budget = min(0.10 * n + 3.0, max(2.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 30.0))
                    a_ad = adam_smooth(a_lp, time.monotonic() + adam_budget)
                    if a_ad is not None:
                        s_ad = candidate_score(a_ad)
                        pool = add_candidate(pool, s_ad, a_ad)
                        if s_ad < best_score:
                            best_score = float(s_ad)
                            best_seq = a_ad.copy()
                            print("new best after Adam:", float(s_ad), "len:", int(n))

    # Medium length 512: usually a sweet spot for LP cost vs. approximation quality.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 160.0:
        n = 512
        starts = make_starts(n, rng)
        scored = []
        for a in starts:
            s = candidate_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(s):
                scored.append((s, a))
                pool = add_candidate(pool, s, a)
                if s < best_score:
                    best_score = float(s)
                    best_seq = a.copy()
                    print("new best:", float(s), "len:", int(n))

        # Also seed 512 from the current global best if its length differs.
        if best_seq is not None and best_seq.size != n:
            a_up = upsample_seq(best_seq, n)
            a_up = normalize_to_sum(a_up, math.sqrt(2.0 * n))
            if a_up is not None:
                s_up = candidate_score(a_up)
                scored.append((s_up, a_up))
                pool = add_candidate(pool, s_up, a_up)
                if s_up < best_score:
                    best_score = float(s_up)
                    best_seq = a_up.copy()
                    print("new best after upsample:", float(s_up), "len:", int(n))

        top = select_top_sequences(scored, 3)
        for s0, a0 in top[:2]:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 120.0:
                break
            budget = min(130.0, max(20.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 80.0))
            if budget < 20.0:
                break
            a_lp = lp_improve(a0, time.monotonic() + budget, max_solves=20)
            if a_lp is not None:
                s_lp = candidate_score(a_lp)
                pool = add_candidate(pool, s_lp, a_lp)
                if s_lp < best_score:
                    best_score = float(s_lp)
                    best_seq = a_lp.copy()
                    print("new best after 512 LP:", float(s_lp), "len:", int(512))

            if time.monotonic() < deadline - 80.0:
                adam_budget = min(60.0, max(15.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 50.0))
                a_ad = adam_smooth(a_lp, time.monotonic() + adam_budget)
                if a_ad is not None:
                    s_ad = candidate_score(a_ad)
                    pool = add_candidate(pool, s_ad, a_ad)
                    if s_ad < best_score:
                        best_score = float(s_ad)
                        best_seq = a_ad.copy()
                        print("new best after 512 Adam:", float(s_ad), "len:", int(512))

    # If the previous Raman / height sequence is available, use it as a large start.
    hs = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if hs is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 150.0:
        hs = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if hs.size > 2 and hs.size <= 20000 and np.all(np.isfinite(hs)):
            s_hs = candidate_score(hs)
            pool = add_candidate(pool, s_hs, hs)
            if np.isfinite(s_hs):
                if s_hs < best_score:
                    best_score = float(s_hs)
                    best_seq = hs.copy()
                    print("new best from height_sequence_1:", float(s_hs), "len:", int(hs.size))
                # Polish only if it is competitive and we have enough time.
                if s_hs < 1.9 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 150.0:
                    ad_budget = min(120.0, max(30.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 100.0))
                    a_hs = adam_smooth(hs, time.monotonic() + ad_budget)
                    if a_hs is not None:
                        s_hs2 = candidate_score(a_hs)
                        pool = add_candidate(pool, s_hs2, a_hs)
                        if s_hs2 < best_score:
                            best_score = float(s_hs2)
                            best_seq = a_hs.copy()
                            print("new best after height Adam:", float(s_hs2), "len:", int(a_hs.size))

    # Final large length attempt: 1024, if enough time remains.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 260.0:
        n = 1024
        starts = make_starts(n, rng)
        scored = []
        for a in starts:
            s = candidate_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(s):
                scored.append((s, a))
                pool = add_candidate(pool, s, a)
                if s < best_score:
                    best_score = float(s)
                    best_seq = a.copy()
                    print("new best:", float(s), "len:", int(n))

        if best_seq is not None and best_seq.size != n:
            a_up = upsample_seq(best_seq, n)
            a_up = normalize_to_sum(a_up, math.sqrt(2.0 * n))
            if a_up is not None:
                s_up = candidate_score(a_up)
                scored.append((s_up, a_up))
                pool = add_candidate(pool, s_up, a_up)
                if s_up < best_score:
                    best_score = float(s_up)
                    best_seq = a_up.copy()
                    print("new best after upsample to 1024:", float(s_up), "len:", int(n))

        top = select_top_sequences(scored, 2)
        for s0, a0 in top[:1]:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 180.0:
                break
            budget = min(220.0, max(30.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 120.0))
            if budget < 30.0:
                break
            a_lp = lp_improve(a0, time.monotonic() + budget, max_solves=12)
            if a_lp is not None:
                s_lp = candidate_score(a_lp)
                pool = add_candidate(pool, s_lp, a_lp)
                if s_lp < best_score:
                    best_score = float(s_lp)
                    best_seq = a_lp.copy()
                    print("new best after 1024 LP:", float(s_lp), "len:", int(1024))

            if time.monotonic() < deadline - 100.0:
                adam_budget = min(90.0, max(20.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 70.0))
                a_ad = adam_smooth(a_lp, time.monotonic() + adam_budget)
                if a_ad is not None:
                    s_ad = candidate_score(a_ad)
                    pool = add_candidate(pool, s_ad, a_ad)
                    if s_ad < best_score:
                        best_score = float(s_ad)
                        best_seq = a_ad.copy()
                        print("new best after 1024 Adam:", float(s_ad), "len:", int(1024))

    # Use remaining time to polish the current best and try a few extra random starts.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 40.0 and best_seq is not None:
        ad_budget = min(160.0, max(20.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 30.0))
        a_ad = adam_smooth(best_seq, time.monotonic() + ad_budget)
        if a_ad is not None:
            s_ad = candidate_score(a_ad)
            pool = add_candidate(pool, s_ad, a_ad)
            if s_ad < best_score:
                best_score = float(s_ad)
                best_seq = a_ad.copy()
                print("new best after final Adam:", float(s_ad), "len:", int(a_ad.size))

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 35.0:
        # A few quick random local searches around the best sequence.
        n_extra = int(best_seq.size) if best_seq is not None else 512
        n_extra = min(max(64, n_extra), 2048)
        starts = make_starts(n_extra, rng)
        scored = []
        for a in starts:
            s = candidate_score(a)
            if np.isfinite(s):
                scored.append((s, a))
                pool = add_candidate(pool, s, a)
                if s < best_score:
                    best_score = float(s)
                    best_seq = a.copy()
                    print("new best in final random start:", float(s), "len:", int(n_extra))

        top = select_top_sequences(scored, 2)
        for s0, a0 in top[:1]:
            budget = max(5.0, deadline - time.monotonic() - 20.0)
            if budget > 5.0:
                a_ad = random_block_search(a0, time.monotonic() + min(30.0, budget), rng)
                if a_ad is not None:
                    s_ad = candidate_score(a_ad)
                    pool = add_candidate(pool, s_ad, a_ad)
                    if s_ad < best_score:
                        best_score = float(s_ad)
                        best_seq = a_ad.copy()
                        print("new best after final block search:", float(s_ad), "len:", int(n_extra))

    # Final verification with the authoritative evaluator.
    final_seq = [1.0, 2.0]
    final_score = float("inf")

    # Prefer to test pool members with moderate length first.
    pool_sorted = sorted(pool, key=lambda iv: (iv[1].size > 20000, iv[0])) if pool else []

    for s_internal, a in pool_sorted:
        if a is None or a.size < 2 or a.size > 50000:
            continue
        try:
            s_exact = evaluate_sequence(a.tolist())
        except Exception:
            s_exact = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(s_exact) and s_exact < final_score:
            final_score = float(s_exact)
            final_seq = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)]

    # If verification failed or was worse than fallback, use best_seq, then [1,2].
    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        try:
            s_exact = evaluate_sequence(np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64).tolist())
        except Exception:
            s_exact = float("inf")
        if np.isfinite(s_exact):
            final_score = float(s_exact)
            final_seq = [float(max(0.0, min(1000.0, x))) for x in np.asarray(best_seq, dtype=np.float64)]

    if not np.isfinite(final_score):
        final_seq = [1.0, 2.0]

    print("internal incumbent best:", float(best_score) if np.isfinite(best_score) else "inf")
    print("verified final best:", float(final_score) if np.isfinite(final_score) else "inf")

    return final_seq
```
